:CREATED:  [2026-09-09 Wed 14:36]
:CATEGORY: Queue
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-09 Wed 14:32]--[2026-09-09 Wed 14:37] =>  0:05
- [2026-09-09 Wed 14:32]--[2026-09-09 Wed 14:37] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-09 Wed 14:43]--[2026-09-09 Wed 14:47] =>  0:04
- [2026-09-09 Wed 14:43]--[2026-09-09 Wed 14:47] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-09 Wed 14:49]--[2026-09-09 Wed 14:53] =>  0:04
- [2026-09-09 Wed 14:49]--[2026-09-09 Wed 14:53] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-09 Wed 14:56]--[2026-09-09 Wed 15:01] =>  0:05
- [2026-09-09 Wed 14:56]--[2026-09-09 Wed 15:01] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-09 Wed 15:04]--[2026-09-09 Wed 15:06] =>  0:02
- [2026-09-09 Wed 15:04]--[2026-09-09 Wed 15:06] assigned at review
:END:
:PLAN:
Caught by the user 2026-09-09, mid-review: a span [08:30]--[13:36]
kept reappearing, wider each pass, overlapping CLOCK lines already
applied. Diagnosed from the queue files: session =5b42fc84='s stream
opens =clock_in "Review and planning"= at 08:30:07 and the next clock
event is =clock_in b0e478f7= 100 seconds later — no =clock_out= for
the first ever exists, because at apply org's own =org-clock-in=
closes the running clock, so the session had no reason to send one.
The queue's bracket pairing is per heading, so on the queue side that
=clock_in= is an *unmatched bracket*, which
=--review-items-from-queue= deliberately reads as "work still
running": its span re-derives to the newest guidepost on every pass,
and nothing consumes the event — after four applies (62 events in the
watermark) 08:30:07 was still absent from the applied set.

The fix candidates, in preference order: (1) at pairing time, a later
=clock_in= in the *same lane* closes an open bracket at its own
timestamp — mirroring exactly what org does at apply, so the queue's
model matches the record the apply will produce; (2) failing that,
the unmatched-clock_in span should *end* at the lane's next
=clock_in=, not at the newest guidepost, which stops the growth but
still re-offers forever. The double-count guard held, for the
record: the span's runs subtract owned brackets (=eaeeb4ee=), so the
overlap was envelope-level; the defect is the unkillable, growing
item, not double-written time.
:END:

A session that clocks into a new heading without closing the previous
one leaves an eternally open bracket on the queue side — org closes
the clock fine at apply, but the review re-derives the unmatched
=clock_in='s span to the newest guidepost on every pass, growing and
never consumable. Proposed: pairing treats a later same-lane
=clock_in= as closing the open bracket, matching what apply actually
does. Diagnosis in =:PLAN:=.

*Worse than filed, measured 2026-09-09 15:0x when the user accepted
the span:* apply wrote the envelope's runs onto the day node
*including ~45 minutes already credited to headings* — 08:31–08:36
and 08:56–09:14 (=b0e478f7=), 09:24–09:28 (=e396f94a=), 09:50–10:02
(=78a02667=/=9d009401=), 11:34–11:41 (=9d009401=), and 13:25–13:36
overlapping =85d24dba=. Permission blocks *were* subtracted; sibling
clock brackets were not, and consumed guideposts were re-offered — so
the =eaeeb4ee= guard covers the orphan span path but NOT this
unmatched-clock_in residue path, which reads full history. The defect
is therefore not only an unkillable growing item: accepting it
double-writes. The session's earlier analysis asserted the opposite
("runs subtract owned brackets", "emission is pending-only") and both
claims are falsified for this path; the day-node record was hand
-corrected by the user afterwards. Fix priority rises accordingly:
the same-lane-close pairing fix in this =:PLAN:= would have made the
item a normal bracket and prevented the whole class.


* TODO session-context.sh reports which groupings lack a NEXT, never which headings hold one
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       81eaf26f-97ae-4dbf-acf5-e457083ab9f9
:CREATED:  [2026-09-04 Fri 13:09]
:CATEGORY: Meta
:END:

=claude-code-ide-org-session-context= assembles three things: the clocked
heading, =--attention-headings-context=, and =--nomination-candidates-context=.
The last reports **absences** — groupings whose live members include no =NEXT=.
Nothing reports the nominations that *exist*.

*So a session starts knowing where a decision is missing and not knowing what
was decided.* On 2026-09-04 the corpus held four top-level =NEXT=s across four
categories — one per category, which is what =758a8b78= caps them at — and none
appeared at session start. That roster is the shortest honest answer to "what
was I doing", and this hook's own charter is literally *"what was I last
doing"*.

**Not the same defect as =4133772c= or =43aa25b2=, and worth keeping apart.**
Those two are *gaps*: a =REVIEW= heading or a stale =DOING= story is something
wrong that nothing surfaces. A =NEXT= sitting there is **correct** — nothing is
broken, and reporting it is orientation rather than a nag. Same hook, opposite
purpose, and conflating them would put a warning register around a line that
should read as a summary.

*Which also settles the volume question the other two have to argue about.* One
per category is a hard cap, so the roster is bounded at the number of live
categories — four today, ten at most — and it does not grow with the corpus.

*Filed 2026-09-04 after being dropped.* It was proposed alongside =4133772c=,
the reply named only "the first thing", and it fell out. Recorded because the
omission was an artefact of a precise instruction rather than a decision, which
is the failure this whole sweep exists to catch.


* TODO A DOING story is invisible to every report, and two have sat a fortnight
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       4133772c-b81d-46e3-8cf7-98359f9a6526
:CREATED:  [2026-09-04 Fri 12:51]
:CATEGORY: Meta
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-04 Fri 12:51]--[2026-09-04 Fri 12:52] =>  0:01
- [2026-09-04 Fri 12:51]--[2026-09-04 Fri 12:52] file the DOING-story gap; narrow 2c3aadbe
:END:

=43aa25b2= one state over. =--attention-headings-context= reports a =DOING=
*leaf* the file and the queue disagree about, and its clause opens =(and (equal
state "DOING") (not (claude-code-ide-org--grouping-heading-p)))= — so a =DOING=
**story** is excluded by construction. That exclusion is deliberate and right in
its own terms: a story is =DOING= because a member is in the mail, and nagging
about it every session would be wrong. The *effect* is that nothing surfaces one
at all, ever.

*Measured 2026-09-04. Every live =DOING= heading is a story, and two are old:*

| heading                                            | own last stamp | |
|----------------------------------------------------+----------------+------|
| =4c834fdb= scannable summary                        | 2026-09-02     | 2 d  |
| =02aaae22= update CLAUDE.md and the org skill       | 2026-09-02     | 2 d  |
| =420816ec= how the emacs-tools are advertised       | **2026-08-21** | 14 d |
| =406e9200= guidepost pipeline durations             | **2026-08-18** | 17 d |

*Found the same way =43aa25b2= was — by a human noticing.* The user asked
whether =420816ec= had been overlooked "just like the REVIEWs", and it had:
fourteen days, three of its findings already measured, and no mechanism that
would ever mention it. =406e9200= is older still and nobody has mentioned it
today; it is =[10/13]=, three members from done, and it blocks =0c8644ff=.

**Report-all is wrong here, unlike for =REVIEW=.** A =REVIEW= heading is by
definition waiting and the population is tiny. A =DOING= story is by definition
long-lived, so report-all names four every session including two touched this
week — the noise that trains the eye to skim, which is the failure every report
here is built to avoid. The signal is **staleness**, not existence.

*And the metric is the open question, not the clause.* The table above uses each
story's **own** block. A story whose *members* are moving while its body sits
still would read stale wrongly, and =406e9200= at =[10/13]= is exactly the shape
that might. The honest measure is latest activity across the story **and** its
members, which is more than the existing clause computes — so decide that before
writing the branch. Precedent for the shape: the =DOING=-leaf clause already
uses quiet-since-before-today as its abandonment test; a story wants a longer
window than a day.

*Where it goes:* a fifth clause in =--attention-headings-context=, the same
place =43aa25b2= landed, for the same reason — that function already answers
"what should a starting session be told about", and a forgotten story is that
question. **Not the org skill**: a skill loads on trigger match, possibly never,
and a report that must fire at session start cannot depend on that.


* REVIEW footnote-check resolves every id and discards the answer, then asks for it
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       33c3eabc-00f2-4cf1-afb7-4071aa256d62
:CREATED:  [2026-09-04 Fri 12:24]
:CATEGORY: Dev
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-04 Fri 12:15]--[2026-09-04 Fri 12:19] =>  0:04
- [2026-09-04 Fri 12:15]--[2026-09-04 Fri 12:19] assigned at review
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-09-04 Fri 12:33] \\
  Dev nomination; category held none
CLOCK: [2026-09-16 Wed 09:34]--[2026-09-16 Wed 09:40] =>  0:06
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-09-16 Wed 09:34] \\
  emit the footnote block the hook already computes
- [2026-09-16 Wed 09:34]--[2026-09-16 Wed 09:40] emit id, keyword and title from the hook
CLOCK: [2026-09-16 Wed 09:40]--[2026-09-16 Wed 09:43] =>  0:03
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-09-16 Wed 09:40] \\
  hook emits keyword and exact title
- [2026-09-16 Wed 09:40]--[2026-09-16 Wed 09:43] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-16 Wed 09:43]--[2026-09-16 Wed 09:44] =>  0:01
- [2026-09-16 Wed 09:43]--[2026-09-16 Wed 09:44] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-16 Wed 09:44]--[2026-09-16 Wed 09:46] =>  0:02
- [2026-09-16 Wed 09:44]--[2026-09-16 Wed 09:46] suggested span, accepted at review
- State "DOING"      from "DONE"       [2026-09-16 Wed 14:15] \\
  reopened: emission format retracted
- State "REVIEW"     from "DOING"      [2026-09-16 Wed 14:18] \\
  core claim stands; emission reformatted
:END:
:PLAN:

=bin/hooks/footnote-check= validates a candidate by looking it up in the tracked
org files:

: grep -qE "^:ID: +$id" "${org_files[@]}" || continue   # not a tracked heading

**It finds the line and throws it away.** =-q= reports existence and discards
the location, which is one =awk= from the heading above it — the keyword and the
exact title the block message then demands be "looked up rather than recalled".
The hook performs the lookup, forgets it, and asks for it back.

*Measured over one session, 2026-09-03/04:* **18 firings, 39 flagged ids, 27
distinct, 10 flagged more than once** — so roughly a third of the flags are
re-flags of ids already footnoted earlier in the same conversation. The response
arguing for this heading was itself blocked, on an id cited as vocabulary.

*The fix is not to relax the rule, and that is the point.* Per-response
footnoting is deliberate — =2758f3a0= is titled "Footnote discipline decays on
re-mention" and the re-flag is the feature, since a reader does not have the
earlier response on screen. **Per-response is expensive only because the lookup
is manual.** Have the hook emit the footnote lines in its own message, or append
them outright, and the rule costs nothing per re-mention while its guarantee is
untouched.

*Two shapes, not settled here.* Emitting the block for the model to paste keeps
the hook advisory and the prose the model's own; appending it removes the
failure mode entirely but has the hook writing user-visible text, which no other
hook here does. The first is smaller and preserves the ask-do-not-act contract
every other report keeps.

*It also subsumes the softer option.* Relaxing to per-session re-mention was the
obvious alternative and trades a reader guarantee for a cost that disappears
once the hook does its own arithmetic.

*Filed beside =2a6a1355= rather than instead of it.* That heading adds three
more ways to owe a footnote and would fire *more* — correctly, and cheaply, once
the response is a paste rather than a grep. =:CATEGORY: Dev= because the work is
in =bin/hooks/=; =2a6a1355= is =Skill= because its subject is the discipline.
Same problem, opposite ends.

*The class this belongs to* is =acf46449='s, one layer over: an error is a
report, and a report is not a mechanism. Here the mechanism already computed the
answer.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-09-16, in the shape this body preferred:* the hook **emits** the
footnote lines and stays advisory, rather than appending them itself. =resolve_id=
replaces the =grep -qE= with one =awk= that keeps the heading it was already
standing on, and the block message now ends with ready-to-paste lines —
=`KEYWORD` `id` — Exact title= — one per unfootnoted citation.

*Plain shell deliberately.* This is a =Stop= hook; routing the lookup through
=emacsclient= would make footnoting fail precisely when Emacs is down. That was
not hypothetical today.

*Three bugs on the way, each caught by a test rather than by reading, and the
third is the interesting one.*

- **An end anchor found nothing.** =^:ID:[ \t]+id[ \t]*$= requires the line to
  stop after eight characters, but candidates are *prefixes* and the line
  carries the full uuid. The =grep= being replaced had no end anchor; parity was
  the fix.
- **An apostrophe in an awk comment closed the shell single-quote** wrapping the
  whole program. Syntax error at the awk, reported against a line that looked
  fine.
- **The keyword assertion passed against the OLD hook too.** Asserting the bare
  string =TODO= was satisfied by the hook's own instruction text — "its current
  TODO keyword" — so the test proved nothing and looked green. Sharpened to
  assert the rendered pairing =`TODO` `id`=, which fails against HEAD as it
  should. A vacuous assertion satisfied by boilerplate is this project's
  mutation-test lesson in a new place.

*Both tests proved red against the previous hook*, and the existing 21 still
pass — including the case that caught bug one.

*What this does and does not close, measured against
[[id:2a6a1355-615f-4c2c-a8af-c9ae8dcda09b][2a6a1355]]'s five modes*, since the
user asked whether it subsumes that heading. **It does not.** Enumeration,
quoted example and borrowed prose were already *detected* — the hook refused
them; what it removes there is the cost, which is the whole point. The
paraphrased-title mode becomes **structurally impossible**, because the correct
string is now on screen at the moment the footnote is written, and that was the
only one of the five producing a confidently wrong answer rather than a refusal.
The **marker collision** stays: placement is the one thing a =Stop= hook cannot
fix, since it may refuse and inform but never rewrite. So =2a6a1355= stays open
for its marker-bookkeeping half, which is a different change to a different part
of the same message.

*Reopened and partly retracted 2026-09-16: **the emission format changed, the
central claim did not.***

This heading's point — the hook resolves every id to check it is tracked and
then throws the answer away, asking the model to look up what it had just
computed — stands unchanged and is now the *only* thing the hook does beyond
the presence check. What it emits, however, has been rewritten: the markers
that came with it are gone (=2a6a1355=).

*What was wrong with the shipped form.* It emitted =⁵ `TODO` `id` — title=,
numbering each line so a pasted marker could not collide. That solved a problem
the convention never posed: =~/.claude/CLAUDE.md= mentions superscripts zero
times. The numbering was careful machinery for an accretion.

*The form now:* after a =---= separator, sorted by identifier, =`id`= then the
keyword padded to a column then the exact title. **No markers, and no numbering
to get wrong.**

*Which retires most of what this heading's follow-on built* — =sup_digit=,
=num_to_sup=, =marker_n= and the orphan check, 189 lines removed against 16
added. The encoding bug recorded above (literal superscripts losing every
three-byte character) is now a bug in deleted code; it is kept in this body
because the *lesson* outlived the code: 23 tests passed while the hook emitted
lines with no marker at all, because not one of them looked.

*One thing the rewrite confirms about the original.* The emission was already
sorted by id — =sort -u= on the candidates does it — so the convention's
"ordered by first appearance" had never described the output. The format change
made the mechanism and the rule agree for the first time rather than changing
what the mechanism did.


* TODO --review-format-annotation's docstring contradicts its own let*, four lines down
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       60ed5b96-40e3-4676-a742-07a8ad6cc3b7
:CREATED:  [2026-09-04 Fri 11:37]
:CATEGORY: Apply
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- [2026-09-04 Fri 11:31]--[2026-09-04 Fri 11:35] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-04 Fri 11:36]--[2026-09-04 Fri 11:37] =>  0:01
- [2026-09-04 Fri 11:36]--[2026-09-04 Fri 11:37] correct two amendments; the toggle already exists
:END:

=claude-code-ide-org--review-format-annotation='s docstring says **"Always
/inactive/ timestamps, so nothing written from the queue reaches
=org-agenda="**, and closes with "there is no case in which a queue-derived span
is the user's own attention, and no branch to make". Its own =let*=, four lines
below the docstring, is:

: (fmt (if (plist-get item :active) "<%Y-%m-%d %a %H:%M>" "[%Y-%m-%d %a %H:%M]"))

Both were true when written. The branch was removed for keying on =:agent= — a
field that tested "is this a subagent" while claiming to test "is this a human"
— and **re-added 2026-08-24** keyed on something else entirely: the bracket
style a human types at =claude-code-ide-org-review-edit-interval='s prompt. The
removal argument was about *inference*; the re-addition is *assertion*. The
docstring never learned the difference.

*Filed because of what it cost, not because it is untidy.* On 2026-09-04 this
sentence misled a reading of that function **twice in one day, in opposite
directions** — once into recording that active marking would "reverse a measured
decision" (it would not; half of it is built), and once into telling the user
the review buffer has no kind toggle (it has had one for eleven days). Both
notes had to be corrected, on =96a51c2f= and =01849bef=. The code was four lines
away both times.

*This is CLAUDE.md's own thesis arriving inside the module rather than in
prose:* "a stale claim here outranks the truth until someone deliberately
checks", and the docstring is loaded into the reader's attention exactly the way
CLAUDE.md is. The repair is small — the docstring should say what the =:active=
flag means, who sets it, and that inference from queue fields is what was
rejected — but the class is worth a moment's thought, since
=bin/check-conventions= checks documents against the code and nothing checks a
docstring against the function under it.


* TODO Nothing lays out future work on a timeline the agenda can show
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       ecf66d45-18b3-477f-bca8-08bec90604e8
:CREATED:  [2026-09-04 Fri 08:52]
:CATEGORY: Skill
:BLOCKER:  b0e478f7-e894-4ac7-b0b6-b3c890b5b32a
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-04 Fri 08:22]--[2026-09-04 Fri 08:51] =>  0:29
- [2026-09-04 Fri 08:22]--[2026-09-04 Fri 08:51] assigned at review
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-09-08 Tue 08:39] \\
  nominated on closing 84b7d8b3; Skill's slot is free
- State "TODO"       from "NEXT"       [2026-09-08 Tue 09:44] \\
  demote the misapplied nomination; deferred to packaging
:END:

*Wanted by the user 2026-09-04, stated as a requirement for future projects
rather than a gap in this one:* "In future projects I will want to be able to
lay out a timeline into the future visible in the agenda."

*Measured on arrival, because the answer is starker than expected.* Across
=TODO.org= and =DONE.org=: **3 =SCHEDULED:= lines and 0 =DEADLINE:= lines.** All
three schedule *recurrences*, not work — the daily archive and day-node ceremony
tasks (=++1d=) and =96a51c2f='s monthly re-measure (=+1m=). **Not one heading in
this project has ever been scheduled to say when it will be done.** Sequencing
is carried entirely by slices, which order work but say nothing about *when*,
and by =:BLOCKER:=, which says what must precede what but not on which day.

So the agenda shows this project's recurrences and nothing else. A forward view
— what lands this week, what slips — has no input.

*What makes this more than "start using =SCHEDULED:=".* The active/inactive
timestamp distinction is already load-bearing here and points the other way:
=b8e6007a= settled that the queue records agent activity and the agenda must not
absorb it, which is why span timestamps are inactive. An agenda that starts
carrying *forecasts* has to keep them apart from the *record* — a scheduled date
is a claim about the future and a CLOCK line is a measurement of the past, and
the agenda renders both. Deciding that boundary before populating it is the
work, not typing dates.

*Two open questions, neither settled here.* Whether a slice gains dates at all,
or only its members — a slice sequences without committing to a calendar, and
dating one may be the wrong shape. And whether this is a convention for *this*
repo or belongs in the portable half, since the ask was framed as "future
projects": that is =9ae4b17e='s classification question arriving from a new
direction, and it is why this carries =:CATEGORY: Skill= rather than =Meta=.

*Deferred to packaging, 2026-09-08 (the user), and the nomination
withdrawn* — a =NEXT= queued minutes earlier on closing =84b7d8b3= is
to be declined at review. The heading's own last question ("this
repo's convention or the portable half's?") is answered by the
deferral: it is decided *with* the portable half, so
[[id:b0e478f7-e894-4ac7-b0b6-b3c890b5b32a][b0e478f7]] is now its
=:BLOCKER:=. The forecast-vs-record boundary design happens there.

*Answered 2026-09-11 by a delegated survey, for the question this heading must
settle before it dates anything: does org cascade a slipped schedule down a
dependency chain — task 1 overruns, and 2, 3, 4 bump forward in turn?* **No,
nowhere.** Not org core, org-contrib, GNU/NonGNU ELPA or MELPA; a sweep of all
6,304 MELPA packages for =gantt|critical.?path|pert|cpm|precedence= returned
zero. Org has no "a date changed" hook at all, so any cascade must be *invoked*
— a bulk agenda command or an explicit tool — never fired.

*Four things get mistaken for it.* =ORDERED=, org-depend and org-edna =BLOCKER=
all gate **state**, never dates. org-depend's single date action,
=chain-siblings-scheduled=, *copies* the predecessor's scheduled time to its
next sibling instead of shifting it — the opposite of what an overrun needs —
and walks siblings only, never =:BLOCKER:=. =ox-taskjuggler= genuinely solves
the cascade, but as a one-directional export with no importer, and it needs
=Effort=, of which this corpus has **zero**; it has also left org core (9.5) and
then org-contrib, and now lives in a third-party repo. org-gantt and elgantt
compute dates internally and discard them.

*The only real primitive anywhere is org-edna's* =scheduled!("+Nd")=, which
shifts a target's own date relative to itself. Two limits keep it a primitive
rather than the feature: it fires **only** on a transition to DONE, and it
advances exactly **one hop** — =todo!= re-enters =org-trigger-hook= and so
chains, while the date actions call =org--deadline-or-schedule= directly and
fire nothing. No finder walks a dependency graph either; the shortest credible
build is a custom finder returning the transitive =:BLOCKER:= closure, with the
shipped =scheduled!= doing the arithmetic.

*Three hazards for this heading's own forecast-vs-record boundary,* which is why
this belongs here rather than on a tooling heading. =SCHEDULED=/=DEADLINE= are
active timestamps by construction — correct for a forecast, but it means the
line to defend is that nothing *derived from clock measurement* ever becomes
one. =org-log-reschedule= and =org-log-redeadline= are both nil today and should
stay nil: either would write revision lines into =:LOGBOOK:=, the same drawer
the CLOCK record lives in. And any cascade hung on =org-trigger-hook= would fire
**during the human apply pass**, writing forecast dates in bursts and bypassing
the queue outright — the queue has no event type for a proposed date change.

*Provenance, stated because it bounds how far this should be trusted:* the
org-edna behaviour above comes from reading its source, not from running it. It
is not installed here.


* TODO No tool edits a heading's title, so a rename goes behind Emacs or not at all :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       24c7b537-7fd1-4327-86af-3807ddf36ebe
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 12:24]
:CATEGORY: Tools

Found 2026-09-03 when a design revision left :ID: 758a8b78's title stating the
withdrawn half of its own proposal. org_amend is body-only, the Edit tool writes
behind a live buffer, and the one honest path was a hand emacsclient form —
which the read-only buffer then correctly refused, proving CLAUDE.md's point
that reaching for emacsclient against a read-only buffer signals a missing tool.
The rename went through as a one-off with inhibit-read-only bound, the tools'
own pattern. Proposed: a small org_rename tool — :ID: plus new title, wrapping
org-edit-headline, which preserves keyword, priority, cookie, and tags. Note the
knock-on: slice member lines copy referent titles, so a rename leaves stale
copies until refresh-slice runs; the tool's reply should say when the renamed
heading appears in slice member lines, or the tool should nudge the refresh
itself.


* TODO org_query answers from a restricted or short file set without saying so :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b79b6b78-0bb0-47f2-b84c-a5d969f65252
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 09:24]
:CATEGORY: Tools
:END:

Found 2026-09-03 while re-checking whether org_query reaches DONE.org. The tool
honors its documented scope — claude-code-ide-org-query-files, falling back to
the org-agenda-files function — but that function's answer is shaped by live
Emacs state the caller cannot see: an agenda restriction lock
(org-agenda-restrict, set by C-x C-< or the dispatcher's < key) silently
collapses it to one file, and a dangling path in org-agenda-files (an entry
whose symlink was never created) silently contributes nothing. Both produced "No
matches" indistinguishable from a true empty result, across three diagnostic
layers in one sitting. Proposed: org_query's reply (and ideally
org_clock_report's and the recovery scan's) should name the file set it actually
searched, and say when a restriction lock is active — "No matches (searched
TODO.org; agenda restriction active)" turns a silent wrong answer into an
inspectable one.


* DOING [5/6] Make the heading body a scannable summary, with plan and debrief in drawers
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       4c834fdb-5314-4168-ab1d-09fe89ded517
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 16:00]
:CATEGORY: Meta
:END:

A heading's body grows without bound, so orientation costs more the longer a
task runs — the median live body is 50 lines against a file of ~118,000 tokens.
The group's thesis is that the body should be a *fixed-size* artifact: a brief
statement of problem and proposal, later a brief resolution, with the reasoning
in =:PLAN:= and the outcome in =:DEBRIEF:=. Members cover the two conventions,
the two tool changes they need, and the retroactive repair of the corpus written
before them.

Precipitated by division 2026-09-02, out of
[[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]], once the fourth member
was filed. Its origin findings stay outside the group:
[[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]] measured that the seams
are not mechanically recoverable, and
[[id:79a3d89e-77be-4678-919e-637a65f87204][79a3d89e]] shipped the nudge that
stops new ones being created.


** DONE Write the plan into :PLAN: from the start, so no seam is ever created :code:
CLOSED: [2026-09-02 Wed 16:02]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 15:19]
:CATEGORY: Meta
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-02 Wed 15:18]--[2026-09-02 Wed 15:20] =>  0:02
- [2026-09-02 Wed 15:18]--[2026-09-02 Wed 15:20] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-02 Wed 15:44]--[2026-09-02 Wed 15:49] =>  0:05
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-09-02 Wed 15:44] \\
  document the drawer purpose shift
- [2026-09-02 Wed 15:44]--[2026-09-02 Wed 15:49] adjust rules and tool descriptions
CLOCK: [2026-09-02 Wed 15:59]--[2026-09-02 Wed 16:00] =>  0:01
- [2026-09-02 Wed 15:59]--[2026-09-02 Wed 16:00] adjust rules and tool descriptions
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-09-02 Wed 16:02] \\
  convention adopted and documented
:END:
:PLAN:

*The user's proposal, 2026-09-02, offered as a simplification after
[[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]] showed the backlog's seams
are not mechanically recoverable:* write the prospective text into =:PLAN:= from
the beginning; keep the task body to a brief problem-and-proposed-solution of
2-5 sentences; append the debrief at DONE-queued time.

*It dissolves the seam rather than solving it.* Every mechanism tried so far —
=org_wrap_plan='s =until=, the lexical marker, the queued-DONE nudge — exists
because prospective and retrospective prose accumulate in one flat body and must
be told apart *later*. Writing them to different places at the moment each is
composed means there is never a mixture, so nothing has to be separated and no
judgement is ever owed. The nudge shipped hours earlier
([[id:79a3d89e-77be-4678-919e-637a65f87204][79a3d89e]]) becomes a *backstop* for
headings that did not follow the discipline, rather than the primary mechanism.

*Verified adoptable with today's tools, before proposing anything be built.*
Four existing calls produce the shape exactly, measured in a scratch file:

: org_amend <design>  →  org_wrap_plan (no until)  →  org_amend <2-5 sentences>  →  [at DONE] org_amend <debrief>

=org_wrap_plan= with no =until= wraps the whole body, which is precisely right
when the body is *still purely prospective* — the case its docstring already
names. =org_amend= then appends below the drawer, which =org_wrap_plan='s own
tool description already promises. So this needs no new tool, and this heading
was written that way.

*The one real tension, and it must be written down or live plans go invisible.*
=:PLAN:= currently means *superseded* design that a reader is told to skip —
=org_body='s description says so explicitly. Under this convention a *live*
heading's drawer holds its **current** plan, which the standing advice would
have readers skip exactly when they most need it. The rule has to become: **skip
=:PLAN:= on a finished heading, read it on a live one.** That is arguably
cleaner than today's, since the drawer's status then follows the heading's
keyword rather than being a property of the drawer, but it is a change and not a
clarification.

*A second, smaller consequence worth naming.* CLAUDE.md's
[[id:b5f94b88-76e3-4a3c-ac73-729f34d33691][b5f94b88]] note says the convention
"does not empty the body of a planned heading", citing reasoning and an incident
record that live in that body. Those are retrospective, so they stay — but they
mean the 2-5 sentence limit governs the body *before* the debrief, not the body
forever. Worth stating, because read as an absolute cap it would push debriefs
into the drawer, which is the original inversion again.

*The payoff beyond the seam is context cost, and it is large.* CLAUDE.md records
the median active heading body at 50 lines. A 2-5 sentence body means
=org_outline= and =org_body= reads become cheap for the *live* set, which is the
set sessions actually read for orientation. That is the same goal as
[[id:9d009401-93dd-40e5-a243-7a30a8a5bd55][9d009401]] approaching from a
different direction.

*What is undecided and belongs to the user:* where the rule lives (CLAUDE.md's
state-transition section, =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md=, or the org skill),
and whether a lint should enforce the cap — a live heading with a body over some
threshold and no =:PLAN:= drawer, which is the exact mirror of the
finished-heading rule that already exists. Both are cheap; neither should be
guessed at.

*Explicitly out of scope:* the 64-heading backlog. This convention stops new
seams being created and does nothing for existing ones, which remain
[[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]]'s three routes.
:END:

Prospective and retrospective prose accumulate in one body and have to be told
apart later, which [[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]]
measured as not mechanically recoverable — the seam is a fact about when a
sentence was written, and nothing in the prose records it. Proposed: write the
plan into =:PLAN:= at the moment it is composed, keep the body to a 2-5 sentence
problem-and-proposed-solution, and append the debrief when DONE is queued. Then
no mixture is ever created and no separation is ever owed. Verified to need no
new tools; the open questions are where the rule lives and whether a lint
enforces the cap.

*Adopted and documented 2026-09-02.* The convention is stated in all four places
it could be contradicted: =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= (canonical section
rewritten), =.claude/skills/org/SKILL.md=, CLAUDE.md and its =org_body=
tool-table row, and the =org_body= / =org_wrap_plan= descriptions in
=config.el=.

*The load-bearing edit was the reading rule, not the writing one.* =:PLAN:=
meant "superseded design, skip it". Under plan-first a *live* heading's drawer
holds its **current** plan, so the old advice would have hidden every live plan
behind an instruction to skip. It now reads: skip on a finished heading, read on
a live one — the drawer's status following the heading's keyword rather than
being a property of the drawer, which is why nothing has to move when a heading
closes.

*Verified adoptable before being written down, and exercised five times since.*
=org_amend= → =org_wrap_plan= → =org_amend= produces the shape with no new
tooling, measured in a scratch file first. Every heading filed after that point
was written this way, including this one.

*Also retired here:* "backlog = wrap unedited", in CLAUDE.md and the skill, with
[[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]]'s measurement as the
reason.

*Residue, filed rather than described:* the three-call workflow is clumsy and
nothing can revise a drawer once written. Both are
[[id:501a8422-9e93-4d2d-98bb-db0ab2c16e37][501a8422]], which would collapse this
to one call.


** DONE Give org_amend a drawer argument, so a plan can be revised and written in one call :code:
CLOSED: [2026-09-08 Tue 12:58]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       501a8422-9e93-4d2d-98bb-db0ab2c16e37
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 15:59]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-09-02 Wed 16:27] \\
  unblocks the one-call plan-first workflow
- State "REVIEW"     from "DOING"      [2026-09-08 Tue 12:39] \\
  shipped and live; open questions answered provisionally
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-09-08 Tue 12:39] \\
  same edit as 635d5abb, same commit
- State "DONE"       from "REVIEW"     [2026-09-08 Tue 12:58] \\
  PR 12 merged at user's direction
:END:
:PLAN:

*The user's proposal, 2026-09-02:* =org_amend= takes a =drawer= argument; null
means the body, as today.

*It is a better idea than the gap that prompted it, and this is the argument for
it.* The gap was small — nothing can revise a =:PLAN:= drawer, so correcting a
plan needs a hand =emacsclient= edit, which
[[id:c8a97d9d-8b13-4b6a-a8b9-1a3f24b5e00b][c8a97d9d]] and CLAUDE.md both warn is
a signal the tool surface is missing something. But the same parameter
**collapses the plan-first workflow from three calls to one**. Today
[[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] requires =org_amend= →
=org_wrap_plan= → =org_amend=; with a drawer argument the first two become
=org_amend drawer=PLAN=. A convention that costs one call instead of three is
materially more likely to be followed, and this one's whole value is that it is
followed every time.

*It also generalises to* [[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]
*for free.* If the debrief moves into =:DEBRIEF:=, that drawer needs exactly the
same write path, and a =drawer= parameter serves it with no further work. A
=org_wrap_debrief= sibling would be a second tool doing the first one's job.

*The safety requirement is specific and non-negotiable.* =:PROPERTIES:= and
=:LOGBOOK:= are **org-managed** — they hold identity, clock lines and state
history, all written by org's own machinery. Appending prose into either would
corrupt the record this project exists to keep trustworthy, and would do it
silently. The tool must refuse them by name, exactly as =org_set_property=
already refuses =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= for the same reason. An allowlist
(=PLAN=, =DEBRIEF=) is safer than a denylist, because the next org-managed
drawer someone adds is then refused by default rather than accepted by omission.

*Two questions genuinely open.* Whether =drawer=PLAN= should *create* the drawer
when absent — which is what makes the one-call workflow possible, and also what
makes it partly subsume =org_wrap_plan=. And whether =replace=true= should be
allowed against a drawer: it is the only way to revise a plan wholesale, and it
is also the most destructive operation in the tool surface pointed at content
readers are told to skip on finished headings, so a bad write there is invisible
by exactly the argument CLAUDE.md already makes about condensing.

*What it does not replace.* =org_wrap_plan='s =until= seam marker stays the
retroactive tool for [[id:35d25265-df52-45c0-8226-dfa24c3ed5bb][35d25265]]'s
pass; nothing here helps with a body that already holds both halves.
:END:

Nothing writes into a =:PLAN:= drawer — =org_amend= appends below it — so
revising a plan needs a hand =emacsclient= edit, which the conventions treat as
a sign the tool surface is short. Proposed: =org_amend= takes a =drawer=
argument, null meaning the body as today. That also collapses
[[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]]'s plan-first workflow from
three calls to one and serves
[[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]'s =:DEBRIEF:= drawer with
no further work. It must refuse =:PROPERTIES:= and =:LOGBOOK:= by allowlist,
since both are org-managed and a silent write there would corrupt the clock and
state record.

*Debrief, 2026-09-08.* Shipped with =635d5abb= (=b1c5541=), carried
into slice =52bfafdf= as its one non-slice member exactly as proposed:
same function, same defect class. =org_amend= takes =drawer=, PLAN or
DEBRIEF by allowlist, refusing =:PROPERTIES:=/=:LOGBOOK:= by the
argument this heading's plan made. The deferred path carries the
target end to end — hook field, event parser, ingestion, review-buffer
rendering (", into :PLAN:"), and =--amend-into-drawer= re-normalises
and re-enforces the allowlist as the one choke point both paths cross,
since a queued event arrives with the raw value.

*The two open questions got provisional answers, decided on this
body's own arguments rather than settled independently.* Create-when-
absent: **yes** — it is what collapses the plan-first composition to
one call, and the plan text itself named that as the point. =replace=
against a drawer: **refused** — the most destructive write in the
surface pointed at content readers skip is the one no later reader
catches; refusing is reversible where allowing is not. Either can be
revisited without breaking the other.

*Verified*: five ERT tests plus two hook assertions, all proven
discriminating against HEAD. Note for callers: a session connected
before this landed has the old tool schema and cannot pass =drawer=
until it reconnects; the apply path honours it immediately.

*The user's proposal, 2026-09-02, alongside
[[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]].* Put the debrief in a
=:DEBRIEF:= drawer placed *below* the body, after adding a brief statement of
the resolution to the body. Folded, a heading then shows: title, a 2-5 sentence
problem-and-proposed-solution, and a one-line resolution — a TL;DR in view while
every drawer and property is collapsed.

*What it completes.* [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] moves
the prospective half out of the body; this moves the retrospective half out too,
leaving the body as *nothing but* the scannable summary. The two together make
the body a fixed-size artifact regardless of how much reasoning or evidence a
task accumulates — which is the property that makes orientation cheap, and the
one neither change achieves alone.

*Ordering, and it is the useful finding here:* this should follow the
retroactive separation ([[id:35d25265-df52-45c0-8226-dfa24c3ed5bb][35d25265]])
rather than precede it. Once the plan is in =:PLAN:=, the remaining body *is*
the debrief, so wrapping it into =:DEBRIEF:= needs no seam judgement at all.
Doing it first would mean deciding two seams instead of one.

*What it costs, stated because it is not free.* The resolution line is
*authored*, not derived — a human or a session must write one per heading, and
for the 64 retroactive headings that is 64 short summaries. Mechanical wrapping
is cheap; the TL;DR is not.

*Open:* whether =org_wrap_plan= generalises to a drawer parameter or a sibling
=org_wrap_debrief= is written; whether the lint should require a resolution line
once a =:DEBRIEF:= exists; and whether =:DEBRIEF:= should be exempt from the
folding advice that tells readers to skip =:PLAN:=, since a debrief on a
finished heading is the thing most worth reading.
:END:

A heading's body still grows without bound once the debrief lands in it, so
orientation stays expensive even after
[[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] moves the plan out.
Proposed: a =:DEBRIEF:= drawer below the body, with a one-line resolution left
in the body — so a folded heading shows problem, proposal and outcome and
nothing else. Should follow
[[id:35d25265-df52-45c0-8226-dfa24c3ed5bb][35d25265]] rather than precede it,
since after the plan is extracted the remaining body *is* the debrief and needs
no seam judgement.

In scope, added 2026-09-03: absorb the pre-archive outcome-summary rule
(CLAUDE.md near line 505, the summary "next to that link"). Post-b75d553a the
link that rule points at sits inside :PLAN:, a drawer readers are told to skip,
and once this task splits outcome recording into a body resolution plus a
:DEBRIEF: drawer, the :505 rule becomes a third description of the same act,
phrased against a location neither artifact will occupy. Rewrite or delete it as
part of landing this, rather than patching it now and rewording it twice.

*Resolved 2026-09-08:* shipped in PR #18 — the debrief now closes into
=:DEBRIEF:= with an authored resolution left in the body, the close
prompt teaches the two grains, and the pre-convention corpus is filed
as its own pass.

Shipped as =9075f8e=. The three open design questions closed as: bodies
*always*, with =bodies=false= as the opt-out (the user: "build it all
now"); the drawer read is a =drawer== parameter on =org_body=, any
drawer name, missing drawers erroring with the drawers actually
present; no rename (=1cd2df62='s churn caution — every doc naming
=org_body= stays true). Body extraction rides
=org-end-of-meta-data FULL=, so :PLAN:, :DEBRIEF:, :LOGBOOK: and
:PROPERTIES: are all excluded from the index — verified by a fixture
whose drawer content must never appear in the outline. One defect
caught mid-build by the suite: an unescaped quote in a docstring
truncated it and left a bare =false= symbol in code position, failing
every outline test with =(void-variable false)= — the kind of break
=check-conventions='s reads-as-forms assertion also guards. Suite
588/588.

*STANDING REMINDER, at the user's request ("tell me when to compact
the context after the corpus pass"): when =e128e4fa= closes, the
pre-convention bodies are short, and whichever session notices should
tell the user it is time to re-measure the unscoped =org_outline= —
and retire the interim =bodies=false= advice in CLAUDE.md's "Reading
the tracker" if the numbers support it.*
:END:
:PLAN:

*The user's proposal, 2026-09-02.* Once the plan and (per
[[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]) the debrief live in
drawers, a session reading for orientation should get the *body* by default and
reach for drawer content deliberately, per item, when it needs depth. The user's
sketch: =org_outline= keeps the body, and =org_body= is refactored — possibly
renamed — to serve on demand what =org_outline= does not.

*Why the current split stops fitting.* =org_outline= today returns one line per
heading with no body at all, and =org_body= returns a heading whole, drawers
included, leaving extraction to the caller. That division made sense when the
body *was* the content: outline for structure, body for everything. Under the
new shape the body becomes a fixed-size summary, so an outline that omitted it
would be withholding the cheapest and most useful thing in the file, while a
=org_body= that returns everything is the expensive call it was designed to
avoid needing.

*The economy argument is measurable rather than aesthetic.* CLAUDE.md records
the median active heading body at 50 lines and TODO.org at ~118,000 tokens. If
bodies become 2-5 sentences, an outline carrying them is affordable for the live
set and answers most orientation questions outright — which is the job
=org_outline= already claims.

*Open, and genuinely undecided:* whether =org_outline= should carry bodies
always, only when scoped, or behind a flag; whether the drawer read is a
parameter on =org_body= (=drawer:PLAN=) or separate tools; and what =org_body=
should be called if its job narrows to "give me the drawer I asked for".
Renaming a tool has its own cost — every skill and rule naming it must move
together, and [[id:1cd2df62-35e9-47dc-bcce-dda7bae3c522][1cd2df62]] is the
standing heading about tool surfaces that lie about what they accept.

*Depends on* [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] and
[[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]: until bodies are actually
short, carrying them in the outline makes it *more* expensive, not less.
Sequencing this first would measure the wrong thing.
:END:

Once plan and debrief move into drawers, =org_outline='s bodiless index
withholds the cheapest useful content while =org_body='s whole-heading read
returns far more than orientation needs. Proposed: =org_outline= carries the
(now short) body, and the drawer read becomes an explicit per-item call — a
refactored, possibly renamed =org_body=. Depends on
[[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] and
[[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]: until bodies are actually
short, putting them in the outline makes it more expensive rather than less.

*Status 2026-09-08:* both named dependencies are now met in the letter
— =b75d553a= and =d5eb32a3= are shipped conventions — but the
precondition in substance is the corpus: bodies become short only as
=e128e4fa= (the 88-heading retroactive pass, filed today) progresses,
and this heading's own plan says sequencing the tool change before
that "would measure the wrong thing". Deliberately left =TODO= behind
=e128e4fa='s nomination; its three open design questions are
unchanged.

Resolved 2026-09-09: built all-now at the user's decision, overriding
this heading's own wait-for-the-corpus sequencing with eyes open —
=org_outline= carries each heading's body beneath its line (drawers
excluded, =bodies=false= to opt out), and =org_body= serves one drawer
on demand via =drawer==, unrenamed. The deferred half is a
*measurement*, not code: see the reminder in =:DEBRIEF:=.

*Authorised by the user 2026-09-02*, after
[[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]] measured that the seams
are not mechanically recoverable and classification opened no escape hatch.

*The instruction, and its three parts.* Accept the absence of a drawer on the
short headings — the 29 below =bin/lint-org='s ten-prose-line threshold are not
a backlog item and never were. Separate plan from debrief on the remaining 64 by
*reading*, using semantics, tense, voice and markup rather than a lexical
marker. And where a heading is *even debatably* ambiguous, wrap the whole body
and leave a =See the :PLAN: drawer for details= note in the body.

*That last clause is a deliberate, bounded exception and must be recorded as
one.* It inverts the convention — it puts a debrief inside a drawer readers are
told to skip — and it is accepted here only because the alternative is a
fabricated seam, which is worse: a wrong cut leaves prospective text standing as
the outcome and buries the debrief's opening, and nothing downstream would ever
notice. **It applies to this retroactive pass only.** No new heading may use it;
[[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] is what makes it
unnecessary going forward.

*What "read it" actually means here*, since "do your best" needs an operational
form: tense (=will= / =should= / =Approach:= versus =shipped= / =verified= /
=measured ... and the result was=), voice (proposing versus reporting), and
markup — this corpus marks prospective framing with =*Proposed by ...*=, =*The
user's proposal*=, =*Decide whether ...*=, and retrospective with =*Shipped
...*=, =*Verified, not just implemented:*=, =*Implementation notes:*=. None of
these is decisive alone, which is exactly what
[[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]] measured; together, on a
heading being read rather than pattern-matched, they usually are.

*Scope check before starting:* 64 warned in DONE.org and 9 in TODO.org. **73 in
total.** Measured rather than assumed: all nine of the TODO.org ones are =DONE=
and merely unarchived, so they are the same case as the 64. An earlier draft of
this plan called them *live*, which the lint's own condition rules out — it
fires only on a finished keyword.

*Ordering:* this precedes [[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]],
and that is the cheap order rather than an arbitrary one. Once the plan is
extracted the remaining body *is* the debrief, so a later =:DEBRIEF:= wrap needs
no second seam judgement.
:END:

64 finished headings carry a mixed body that =bin/lint-org= reports and no
mechanism can split automatically, since the seam is a fact about when a
sentence was written. Proposed: read each one and cut it by tense, voice and
markup; accept no drawer on the 29 short ones; and for any *debatably* ambiguous
heading wrap the body whole with a =See the :PLAN: drawer for details= note — a
bounded exception for this retroactive pass only, never for new headings.

*Complete 2026-09-02. All 73 cut; =bin/lint-org= reports zero =:PLAN:= warnings,
against 73 when the pass began.* Committed in batches of three or four so any
single cut is revertable on its own.

*Integrity checked rather than assumed*, since the pass touched almost every
finished heading in the repo: DONE.org 213 keyworded and 213 ids, TODO.org 134
and 146, no duplicate ids in either. =org_wrap_plan= is lossless by construction
— two insertions, nothing deleted or reflowed — and every call reported =Text
preserved: yes=.

*Four body shapes appeared, and only one needed the exception.*

1. *The ordinary cut* (64). A clean tense flip. Usually a =*Shipped …*=
   paragraph, but the rule that actually held is sharper: **the debrief begins
   where the heading's own job produced its outcome.**
   [[id:ff1352b3-2732-4422-9f0d-4b3cb6fd8ff3][ff1352b3]] was a *decide* heading,
   so its seam is =*Decided …*=;
   [[id:9651d4c8-6887-4b0a-91d9-562c3bc49a75][9651d4c8]] is =CANCELLED=, so its
   seam is =*Cancelled … as superseded by*=;
   [[id:82df2a6c-881d-4117-a752-5b3b04c9656d][82df2a6c]] was an evaluation, so
   its seam is =*Run 2026-08-25.*=. A keyword-matching rule would have missed
   all three.
2. *Debrief-only* (4). [[id:49557477-e50c-496f-85df-82c65109832b][49557477]],
   [[id:e10071b9-88f0-4dc3-af8e-692965c65868][e10071b9]],
   [[id:e1284bdb-0a18-402a-81cb-1ac8d09fb8a2][e1284bdb]] and
   [[id:594adec6-4855-470d-8ceb-5fe29bf0c935][594adec6]] open with their outcome
   and never propose — =e10071b9= was filed and closed a minute apart. These
   take an **empty** =:PLAN:= drawer with the body untouched, which is
   [[id:f421c5c3-f81f-4ebf-99c9-42c53cc00558][f421c5c3]]'s case, and
   =org_wrap_plan= says so in its reply rather than silently wrapping nothing.
3. *Purely prospective* (1).
   [[id:0acc1df2-6916-4180-867a-99bfa551806b][0acc1df2]] closed with **no
   debrief at all** — 27 lines ending on "Manual repair until this is built".
   Whole body wrapped, which is the convention's ordinary completion transition
   rather than the exception. The missing debrief is a pre-existing gap and is
   now recorded on the heading.
4. *The user's exception* (2).
   [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]] and
   [[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]] interleave planning and
   execution across multiple phases — 29439196 over 70 paragraphs, 3063c3e5
   across two ship events with re-scoping between them. No line is the seam.
   Wrapped whole with a pointer note.

*Two of the four shapes were not anticipated when this was filed*, and the
distinction between 2 and 4 is the one worth keeping: **an absent prospective
half is not an unrecoverable seam.** The first keeps the debrief visible; only
the second buries it. Conflating them would have hidden four debriefs for no
reason.

*A side effect worth recording because it was not the goal.* Total lint warnings
fell from 140 to 68. Wrapping pushes many bodies under the ten-prose-line
threshold, so rules unrelated to =:PLAN:= stop firing too — which is a fair
measure of how much of each body was prospective.

*The classification hypothesis is now fully falsified, and the record should say
so.* [[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]] proposed that a
debrief marker on line 1 meant a debrief-only body, offering an escape from
per-heading reading. Across the whole corpus it held for 4 of 73. Reading each
body was the only honest route, exactly as that heading concluded.


** NEXT Move the 88 pre-convention debriefs into :DEBRIEF:, authoring each resolution :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       e128e4fa-b0d9-49f7-99eb-bae2d4b0676b
:CREATED:  [2026-09-08 Tue 16:12]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-09-08 Tue 16:12] \\
  story nomination: it unblocks the remaining member
:END:

The analogue of =35d25265= one drawer over. After that pass extracted
the plans, each finished heading's remaining body *is* its debrief —
so the wrap itself needs no seam judgement, exactly as =d5eb32a3='s
ordering finding predicted. What each heading still needs is an
**authored resolution line**: one-to-two sentences left in the body
before the debrief moves into =:DEBRIEF:=. Measured 2026-09-02, 88 of
93 unwrapped headings carry a debrief, so this is ~88 short summaries
— per-heading judgement, human in the loop or at minimum
human-reviewed in batches, never a blind sweep. Mechanically: for each
finished heading with prose below its drawers, author the resolution,
then move the remainder into =:DEBRIEF:= (=org_amend drawer=DEBRIEF=
serves the write; whether a dedicated seam-free wrapper is worth
building can be decided when the pass starts). This is the
precondition =2a399034= names: until bodies are short corpus-wide,
carrying them in the outline makes it more expensive, not less.

Cross-note 2026-09-09: =2a399034= shipped the read split *before* this
pass, at the user's call — so when this heading closes, tell the user
it is time to re-measure the unscoped =org_outline= (bodies now ride
in the index) and retire the interim =bodies=false= advice in
CLAUDE.md's "Reading the tracker" if the numbers support it. The full
reminder lives in =2a399034='s =:DEBRIEF:=.

*The org conventions were corrected against this heading 2026-09-15,
having been stricter than their own source.* They summarised the
paragraph above as "a per-heading act nobody should do by sweep", where
this body says "human in the loop **or at minimum human-reviewed in
batches**, never a *blind* sweep" — a prohibition where the source
permits a reviewed pass. Caught when the user gave full latitude on this
work and asked why that would be wrong; the honest answer was that the
rule being quoted at them was not the rule. *That is the failure CLAUDE.md
names in its opening section — a claim that loads every session
outranking the artifact it describes — arriving in the conventions rather
than in CLAUDE.md.*

*And the correction added the shape this pass should take*, which was
nowhere written: **three passes, not one.** Relocation is lossless and
verifiable by a non-whitespace digest. Authoring a resolution is additive
and *unverifiable* — =bin/lint-org= can assert one is present, never that
it is true, and a folded heading shows the resolution *instead of* the
body, so a wrong one displaces the record rather than sitting beside it.
Condensation is the pare no later reader catches. So: relocate first and
wholesale, author in reviewable batches, condense last and separately.

*Checked at the same time and needing nothing:* whether
[[id:d350ff5b-3b1f-465e-800b-962264d2e841][d350ff5b]] must precede this. It need
not. =org_amend drawer=DEBRIEF= already writes the drawer in canonical position
— verified on =496b665e=, whose order is =:PROPERTIES:=, =:LOGBOOK:=, =:PLAN:=,
=:DEBRIEF:=, then body — so this pass creates no disorder for that one to clean
up, and the two are independent in either order. The slice lists =d350ff5b=
earlier, which is sequence rather than dependency; no =:BLOCKER:= is warranted.


* TODO A boolean argument passed as false arrives as :false, which elisp reads as true :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       a8ccd6ac-3452-4926-b176-ff5951dcd5b2
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 12:50]
:CATEGORY: Tools
:END:

*Found 2026-09-02 while fixing
[[id:72463b68-9f08-44b2-8213-87f71298138d][72463b68]], noticed in passing and
measured rather than left as a suspicion.*

The MCP server parses a request body with =(json-parse-string body :object-type
'alist)=, whose default =:false-object= is the keyword =:false=. So a JSON
=false= arrives in elisp as the symbol =:false=, and *every non-nil value is
true in elisp*. Measured:

: (json-parse-string "{\"append\":false}" :object-type 'alist)
: => ((append . :false))       ; and (if :false ...) takes the true branch

**The caller gets the opposite of what they asked for**, and only when they are
explicit. Omitting the argument yields =nil= and behaves correctly, so the bug
is invisible to every call that leaves the default alone — which is why it has
never been seen.

Two live arguments, and the second is destructive:

- =org_set_property='s =append=. Passing =false= unions with the existing
  =:BLOCKER:= set instead of replacing it. Wrong, recoverable.
- =org_amend='s =replace=. Passing =false= **replaces the heading's entire
  prose** instead of appending to it. That tool's own description says
  "Destructive and irreversible except through git", and this reaches it by
  asking for the *safe* behaviour.

*Not hypothetical about which tools are exposed:* those are the only two boolean
=:type=s in the surface today, and both were touched on 2026-09-02.

*The fix is one binding, not a per-tool audit.* Parsing with =:false-object nil=
makes JSON =false= arrive as =nil= and read correctly everywhere at once. The
alternative — teaching each handler to recognise =:false= — is the shape this
repo keeps rejecting: a rule applied per site that a later site will forget.
Note the change belongs *upstream* in =claude-code-ide-mcp-http-server.el=
beside [[id:ce012ad4-87f7-435c-a2ee-25210c071865][ce012ad4]], since the parse is
theirs; a local override would have to shadow their function.

*Same family as [[id:6495c8a0-d971-4c77-8b30-9fde1bbf5f26][6495c8a0]]* — a tool
accepting an argument and not honouring it — but with the failure inverted.
There the value is silently discarded; here it is silently *negated*, which is
worse, because discarding leaves the default in place while negating actively
performs the operation the caller declined.


* TODO claude-code-ide-org-capture reports a queued capture it never persists :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       8326a46f-e3f9-4ecc-8bc2-05d54907987b
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 12:09]
:CATEGORY: Tools
:END:

*Split out of [[id:72463b68-9f08-44b2-8213-87f71298138d][72463b68]] on
2026-09-02*, which called this "the dangerous one" and "the last matters most".
Its body holds the original observation; only the residue is restated here.

Called directly over =emacsclient=, outside the MCP wrapper,
=claude-code-ide-org-capture= returned ="Queued capture: … (ID: …) pending
review"= and wrote *nothing* — not to the session queue file, whose mtime never
moved, not elsewhere under =~/.claude/org-updates/=, and not to any org file.
The ids it reported existed nowhere.

*The presumed mechanism, still unverified:* the queue path needs a session
context the MCP wrapper supplies and a bare =emacsclient= call lacks. If that is
right the failure is silent by construction — the caller is told the work is
recorded, which is the worst available outcome and strictly worse than an error.

*The fix is a refusal, not a repair.* A capture that cannot determine where to
queue must say so loudly rather than report success. Whether the =emacsclient=
path should additionally be *made* to queue is a separate and smaller question;
a tool that cannot persist must not claim it did, however it was invoked.

*Why this outlived its parent's fix.*
[[id:72463b68-9f08-44b2-8213-87f71298138d][72463b68]]'s hang is now diagnosed
and fixed — three tools declared a semantically optional argument required, and
the resulting =json-rpc-error= escaped every handler. That fix removes the
*reason* to reach for the =emacsclient= fallback, which is exactly why this one
must not be quietly dropped with it: the trap is now rarer and no less silent,
and rarity is what keeps a false success unnoticed.

2026-09-02*, which found and fixed the hang but left this untouched. It was the
second of that heading's three named fixes.

=(cl-defun claude-code-ide-org-capture (title &optional target tags note
initial-state))= names =note= in its argument list and references it nowhere in

when the write defers", so a caller who supplies one has been told it is kept.

This is [[id:1cd2df62-35e9-47dc-bcce-dda7bae3c522][1cd2df62]]'s "accepts
arguments it does not have, and says nothing" in this project's own

deferred-capture event as the description already claims, or drop the parameter
and the sentence together. What must not survive is the present state, where the
schema advertises a parameter that is silently discarded.

dropped a *body*, and three headings arrived empty because of it. That is the
